# Agent Factory — Design Authority (SSOT)

> **CRITICAL INSTRUCTION FOR AI AGENTS (Claude, Grok, Gemini):**
> This document is the **single, immutable source of truth** for architectural intent, component contracts, and engineering standards across the Agent Factory and Cartridge ecosystem.
> 
> **Zero Code Modification Rule:** No AI agent may create, modify, or delete any codebase files without first documenting the intent, logging the gap, and acquiring an explicit task lock within this document.

---

## 1. Governance & Hierarchy of Authority

When discrepancies arise between repository documents, prompts, or discussions, the following strict order of precedence applies:

1. **`DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md`** (This Document) — The ultimate arbiter of engineering intent, contracts, and lifecycle.
2. **Machine-Enforced Schemas** (`packages/contract/src/schema.ts`, unit tests) — The concrete validator.
3. **`POSITION_PAPER.md`** — Strategic philosophy, north star, and commercial manifesto. In any technical conflict with this document, `DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md` prevails.
4. **AI Platform Instructions** (`CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, Grok system prompts) — Operational guidelines for specific LLMs.

---

## 2. AI Operating Protocol (Mandatory Workflow)

Every AI session (Claude, Grok, or Gemini) **must** follow this strict state machine before and during any code edits.

```
       [User Declares Intent]
                 │
                 ▼
     [AI Updates Intent in DA]
                 │
                 ▼
  [AI Confirms Update with User]
                 │
                 ▼
    [AI Logs Conflict/Gap in DA]
                 │
                 ▼
    [AI Acquires Task Lock in DA]  ◄── (Other AIs blocked on these files)
                 │
                 ▼
      [AI Modifies & Tests Code]
                 │
                 ▼
 [AI Verifies, Closes Task, & Unlocks]
```

### The 6-Step Workflow:

1. **User Declares New Intent:**
   - The user describes a new feature, behavioral modification, or design correction.
2. **AI Updates Design Authority & Confirms:**
   - **First, cite what already applies and test the boundary.** Before proposing any new rule or design, the AI names the existing invariants (§6) and the user's recorded decisions (§7) that bear on it. If an existing invariant already covers the case, the work is to register and fix the violation, not to add a rule. A proposal that contradicts a recorded decision says so explicitly and asks. Crucially, the AI applies **The Donna Test (Cartridge vs. Factory Boundary Gate)**: verify whether the reported defect is a cartridge concern (an agent's reasoning, chat memory, or tool execution) versus a factory concern (compute lifecycle, network perimeter, secrets binding, or mind storage sync). If another agent (like Donna) already succeeds at the behavior, the platform already supports it; the fix belongs in the cartridge or agent template, never in factory kernel packages.
   - The AI updates the *Declared Architectural Intent* section of this document.
   - The AI explicitly confirms this text update with the user before touching code.
3. **AI Documents the Conflict or Gap:**
   - The AI inspects the codebase against the new intent and logs a specific item in the **Conflict & Gap Register** (Section 4).
4. **AI Acquires a Task Lock:**
   - In the **Active Task & Locking Board** (Section 5), the AI creates or transitions the task to `LOCKED`.
   - The AI records: `AI Model (Claude | Grok | Gemini)`, `Timestamp`, `Session ID / Reference`, and `Locked Scope (Target Files)`.
5. **AI Implements Code within Locked Scope Only:**
   - The AI edits only the files claimed in the lock.
   - If adjacent files require changes, the AI must first expand its lock in `DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md`.
6. **AI Closes Task and Releases Lock:**
   - After testing and verification, the AI sets task status to `COMPLETED`, adds resolution notes, and clears the active lock.

---

## 3. Lock Recovery Protocol (Handling Hung or Abandoned Sessions)

Because AI sessions can be terminated unexpectedly (token limits, session crashes, timeouts), a strict lock recovery protocol is required:

1. **Detecting a Stale Lock:**
   - An incoming AI session inspecting the board sees a task marked `LOCKED`.
2. **Verification of Work State:**
   - The incoming AI **must inspect the codebase and git status** for the files listed under `Locked Scope`.
   - Case A: *Work was completed but not marked closed.* The incoming AI runs tests, validates against intent, and marks the task `COMPLETED`.
   - Case B: *Work was partially done or never started.* The incoming AI evaluates whether the existing diff is sound or needs resetting.
3. **Formal Lock Transfer (Override):**
   - The incoming AI explicitly logs in the task history: `[TIMESTAMP] Lock taken over from <Previous AI> by <Current AI> due to inactivity/session end.`
   - The incoming AI updates the `Locked By` field to its own identity and proceeds.

---

## 4. Conflict & Gap Register (Current State vs. Target Intent)

The following gaps exist between current repository code, `SPEC.md`, and the canonical architecture:

| Gap ID | Component | Description of Conflict / Divergence | Severity |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **GAP-001** | **Cartridge Manifest Sprawl** | `SPEC.md` and `packages/contract` require up to 8 separate files (`soul.md`, `surface.yaml`, `secrets.manifest.yaml`, `artifact.yaml`, `bench.yaml`, `skills.yaml`, `identity.yaml`, `memory.yaml`). Several contain only 1–2 lines. Standard practice calls for a single unified `cartridge.yaml` plus `soul.md`. | High |
| **GAP-002** | **Registry API vs Contract Mismatch** | `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts` (`POST /api/v1/registry/agents`) accepts a flat JSON body `{ repo, secrets, triggers }` and invokes AWS CodeBuild, bypassing `packages/contract` and `artifact.yaml` validation entirely. | Critical |
| **GAP-003** | **CaaS vs. PaaS Duality** | `artifact.yaml` expects a pre-built OCI image (`kind: oci, ref: oci://...`), whereas `app.ts` + `codebuild.ts` treats the Factory as a PaaS that builds from Git source on `POST /deploy`. The platform lacks a unified build-and-deploy contract. | Critical |
| **GAP-004** | **Bespoke Worker Invocation Contract** | The reference worker ([worker.mjs](file:///Users/dsackrider/repos/BeerCanLabs/agent-factory/agents/examples/echo-agent/worker.mjs)) polls `GET /api/v1/runs/:id/input` and posts `POST /api/v1/runs/:id/result`. This tightly couples agent logic to the Factory REST API, violating the "portable zero-logic cartridge" principle. Standard CloudEvents/HTTP POST or Stdin/Stdout should be supported. | High |
| **GAP-005** | **Polyglot & Shim Coupling** | The runtime shim (`packages/hydrate/src/shim.ts`) is a Node.js process. Any Python, Go, or Rust agent container is forced to install Node.js 22 runtime dependencies just to execute the shim. | High |
| **GAP-006** | **Position Paper Sidecar Contradiction** | `POSITION_PAPER.md` describes a localhost Envoy sidecar proxy. In reality, `packages/hydrate` sets environment variables pointing across the network to `packages/gatekeeper-egress`. There is no local sidecar proxy intercepting traffic. Documentation and runtime architecture must be aligned. | Medium |
| **GAP-007** | **Mandatory Bench Suite for Registration** | `packages/contract/src/schema.ts` makes `bench.yaml` a mandatory file (`REQUIRED_FILES`). Cartridges cannot pass contract validation without deterministic benchmark cases, impeding simple agent development. | Medium |
| **GAP-008** | **Missing Developer Documentation** | There is no end-to-end "How to Build a Cartridge" guide, SDK reference, or sample template repository for external developers. | High |
| **GAP-009** | **AWS Network Isolation Breach** | `landing-zones/aws/network.tf` routes agent subnets `0.0.0.0/0` to IGW (lines 51-54) and assigns public IPs via `FACTORY_ECS_ASSIGN_PUBLIC_IP: "true"` (ecs.tf line 74). Security group `agents_to_internet` allows all outbound. Agents can bypass the gatekeeper-egress entirely, breaking the core security perimeter promised by the position paper. | Critical |
| **GAP-010** | **GCP Runtime Dead Code** | `FACTORY_RUNTIME=cloudrun` is unhandled in `packages/control-plane/src/index.ts` (falls through to `memoryRuntime`). `gcp/cloudrun.ts` defines `registerAgentJob()` but it is never imported or called. No `runtime-cloudrun.ts` exists. GCP agents cannot wake from zero. | Critical |
| **GAP-011** | **GCP Ledger WORM Crash** | `packages/ledger/src/checkpoints.ts` (line 138) rejects `gcs://` URIs with an unhandled exception. GCP Terraform sets `FACTORY_LEDGER_WORM_URI=gcs://...`, causing the control plane to enter a crash loop on boot. | Critical |
| **GAP-012** | **GCP Mind Hydration Missing** | `packages/hydrate/src/index.ts` only supports `s3://` and local paths. `gcs://` mind URIs set by GCP Terraform are silently ignored. Agent memory is not persisted on GCP. | Critical |
| **GAP-013** | **GCP Secret Manager Missing** | `packages/secrets-bind/src/index.ts` does not implement a GCP Secret Manager provider. `FACTORY_SECRETS_GCP_PROJECT` is set by GCP Terraform but never read by the kernel. | High |
| **GAP-014** | **Hardcoded AWS Imports in Kernel** | `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts` lines 1-3 unconditionally import `aws/ecs.ts`, `aws/iam.ts`, `aws/codebuild.ts`. The `/deploy` handler invokes AWS services regardless of the configured provider, violating cloud-agnosticism in the kernel itself. | Critical |
| **GAP-015** | **Hardcoded AWS Account ID** | `packages/control-plane/src/aws/ecs.ts` line 23 and `aws/codebuild.ts` line 45 contain fallback to account `566332862296`, violating `GEMINI.md` rule prohibiting company-specific AWS account IDs in this repository. | High |
| **GAP-016** | **Training Function Not Operational** | KPF §7 defines the quality evaluation architecture (bench.yaml rubric + prompt traces + trainer cartridge evaluating traces in mind storage), but the evaluation loop is not closed: no differentiated benchmark run mode (`trace: true, model: "pinned"`), no reference trainer cartridge, no automated graduation gate enforcement via the policy engine. The ledger correctly records cost and quantity at the perimeter; quality evaluation is a cartridge concern and belongs in the training function, not the ledger. | Medium |
| **GAP-017** | **Approval Arguments Opaque** | `Approval` stores `argsSha256` but not the actual tool arguments. Human reviewers querying `GET /api/v1/approvals` cannot see what the tool is about to do without separate access to prompt traces. | Medium |
| **GAP-018** | **Cartridge Lifecycle CRUD Incomplete** | No `DELETE /api/v1/registry/agents/:id` or `PUT /api/v1/registry/agents/:id` exists. Agents cannot be updated or decommissioned via the control plane API. | High |
| **GAP-019** | **No API Pagination** | `GET /api/v1/runs` and `GET /api/v1/ledger` lack `limit`, `offset`, or `cursor` parameters. Unusable at scale for reporting dashboards. | Medium |
| **GAP-020** | **No Stdout Streaming Endpoint** | No endpoint to tail container stdout/stderr in real time. UIs must bypass the control plane and connect directly to provider-specific log services (CloudWatch, Cloud Logging, Docker engine), breaking the headless contract. | Medium |
| **GAP-021** | **Stale Docker Blueprint** | `blueprints/docker/docker-compose.yml` is outdated (missing gatekeeper-egress, networks, docker-proxy). Confuses users who find it instead of `landing-zones/compose`. Should be archived or removed. | Low |
| **GAP-022** | **Azure Landing Zone Empty** | `landing-zones/azure/` contains only a 12-line markdown stub. Zero Terraform, Bicep, runtime bindings, or SDK integrations exist. | High |
| **GAP-023** | **Container Images Assume AWS CLI** | All four Dockerfiles (`control-plane`, `gatekeeper-egress`, `gatekeeper-ingress`, `runtimes/generic`) install `aws-cli` via `apk add` regardless of target provider. Dead weight on non-AWS deployments. | Medium |
| **GAP-024** | **No Metrics REST Endpoint** | OTel metrics (`factory.runs.active`, `factory.gatekeeper-egress.cost`, etc.) are recorded internally but no `/metrics` or `/api/v1/metrics` REST endpoint exists for dashboards without an external OTel collector. | Low |
| **GAP-025** | **Agent Memory Architecture** | Referenced by TSK-019 but never registered: cartridges must keep memory in local SQLite under `$MEMORY_DIR`, synced only by the Console shim (§6.6). | High |
| **GAP-026** | **Egress Rule Under-Specified** | §6.3 named only `anthropic`/`openai`, so sessions treated other egress (Bedrock, S3, xAI) as out of scope. `1e55aa7` forced agents through a proxy their SDKs could not authenticate to, breaking all Bedrock calls on AWS from 2026-09-26; a later session proposed bypassing the gatekeeper-egress to fix it. Resolved in intent by §6.3.1 (E1–E6). | Critical |
| **GAP-027** | **Cartridges Use Cloud SDKs** | 8 of 9 `SM-*` cartridges import `boto3` for Bedrock inference and S3 memory, violating §6.6 and E5. TSK-019 was marked `COMPLETED` while this was untrue. Model calls from these cartridges are not metered or ledgered. | Critical |
| **GAP-028** | **No Enforcement of This Document** | CI red on every push since 2026-09-26 (console `TS2367` stops `npm test` at build), core `control plane` test suite `describe.skip` since 2026-09-19 (26/35 fail when enabled: stale fixtures), no branch protection on `main`. Nothing machine-checks any invariant here. | Critical |
| **GAP-029** | **Deploy Pipeline Diverges From Production** | `submind-aws/scripts/aws-deploy.sh` would destroy console and garrison (image vars unset), fails re-running bootstrap, and applies new image tags before building them; production runs `:latest` images registered by hand, so no commit maps to what is running. | High |
| **GAP-030** | **Warm-Down Hot Loop** | `SM-*` warm-down loops ignore the mailbox `done` signal and retry instantly on errors, spinning against the control plane for up to an hour per agent (2026-09-27: three agents drove control-plane CPU from ~1% to ~100%, starving gatekeeper-egress ledger writes). | High |
| **GAP-032** | **Automatic Egress Grants** | `1e55aa7` made the control plane write cartridge-derived routes/hosts plus an `anthropic`/`openai` fallback and the landing-zone `FACTORY_EGRESS_HOSTS` into each agent's policy at registration and at every run start, overwriting admin-set policy. Any agent had model access with no admin decision and no budget, contradicting SPEC.md (deny-by-default, admin-set). Caught by the compose proof on 2026-09-27. | Critical |
| **GAP-033** | **Deploy Role Trusted the Public Repo** | The production `factory-deploy` IAM role trusted GitHub OIDC tokens from `repo:BeerCanLabs/agent-factory:*` (any branch of this public repo) and not the ops repo `submind-aws`, so ops deploys could never assume it while any `agent-factory` branch could. Code said `refs/heads/main` (hand-edited drift); the bootstrap defaulted `github_repo` to this repo. | Critical |
| **GAP-034** | **Agents Baked Into the Platform** | `ea77328` (2026-09-22, inside an unrelated fix) copied the fleet cartridges into `agent-factory/agents/`, and the control-plane image bakes that folder in (`COPY agents`). Production agents exist only because of that copy, bypassing registration. Violates L2. | Critical |
| **GAP-035** | **No Admission Gate** | Registration runs no checks: no pinned commit, no build, no tests, no design-rule checks. The CodeBuild agent builder clones the latest commit. Violates L3. | Critical |
| **GAP-036** | **Unpinned, Hand-Pushed Agent Images** | Agent images are `factory-dynamic-agents:<agent>`, a mutable tag, pushed by hand from a workstation seconds after each commit (2026-09-25); nothing records which commit runs. Violates L4. | High |
| **GAP-037** | **No Agent Version Flow (Alert, Admit, Deploy by a Person)** | L4, L5: the agent-owner role and agent ownership are built (GAP-088); the version flow is not. A new commit on a registered repository is never noticed (the HEAD is resolved once, at registration); admission runs only inside the deploy call, so there is no way to admit without deploying; `registry.deploy` is held by `admin` alone, so an agent's owner cannot deploy it; the console's Deploy button shows only for a `PENDING_DEPLOY` agent and has no "new version available"; and agents are deployed by a GitHub Action in the operations repository (`BeerCanLabs/submind-aws`), outside the factory. Intent (Dale, chat, 2026-10-07): a new commit alerts, an admin admits, a green version shows as "new version available", and the admin or the owner deploys it from the factory; L5 is amended to say so. Plan and open questions: `docs/registrar-roadmap.md`. Not part of the Registrar extraction (GAP-098), which is moves only. | High |
| **GAP-038** | **No Public Agent Template** | The template lives in a private `SM-template` repo (a private naming convention), with the admission requirements undocumented for builders. Violates L6. | Medium |
| **GAP-039** | **Test Fixtures Treated as Agents** | `echo-agent` and `llm-summarizer` sit in `agents/examples/` and appear as agents (echo as built-in since `7db369f`); the compose proof and the AWS definition of done depend on them being in the catalog. | Medium |
| **GAP-040** | **Pre-flight Reads Gatekeeper-Held Keys** | The S1 pre-flight (`0813cb9`) binds every required secret at wake, including provider keys (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) that the landing zone deliberately denies the control plane (`NeverProviderKeys`). Agents declaring one (finley, higgins) would get 412 on every wake. | High |
| **GAP-041** | **No Factory Model API** | Agents call providers directly in provider-specific formats (cloud SDK calls to one cloud's model service, direct calls to another provider) with model IDs hardcoded in agent code; nothing lets operations choose or substitute models, and none of those calls are metered. Violates M1–M3. | Critical |
| **GAP-042** | **Production Images Not Built by CI** | CI never built the production Dockerfiles, and there was no `.dockerignore`, so local builds shipped whatever `dist/` and `node_modules` were on disk. The control-plane Dockerfile compiled `keymaster` before its dependency `ledger`; hand-built images passed only because of stale local output. Found by the first CI deploy (2026-09-28). | High |
| **GAP-043** | **Concurrent Ledger Writers** | Rolling control-plane deploys ran old and new tasks side by side, both appending to the same ledger file on shared storage, tearing and interleaving rows (likely the cause of earlier ledger null-byte, checkpoint-fork, and verification fixes). The production ledger fails verification at seq 288148; the loader also silently skips corrupted rows. Found by the first CI deploy (2026-09-28). | Critical |
| **GAP-044** | **Agents Hold OAuth Grants and App Secrets** | Agents carry OAuth tokens and app client secrets as environment secrets and refresh them themselves; rotated tokens were lost on restart (Donna's health skill kept them only in /tmp), and an app client secret and a shared Notion token were hard-coded in agent repos (Notion exposed publicly via SM-rosie; revoked and rotated 2026-09-28). When consent broke, an agent asked the user to run a script. Violates K1–K4. | Critical |
| **GAP-045** | **No Secret Scanning** | Nothing refuses a repository that hard-codes a credential: not admission, not CI. | High |
| **GAP-046** | **Credentials Are Supplied by Hand** | Agents declare only secret names; the factory cannot tell an owner which credentials are outstanding, how to create them, or accept them safely, so operators store values with CLI commands (the OAuth client, the Notion token). Violates K5. | High |
| **GAP-047** | **No Alerting Design** | The factory has no designed way to notify a person when something needs them (an outstanding credential, a re-consent, a refused admission, a crash). Undesigned; not yet an invariant. | Medium |
| **GAP-048** | **Re-registration Resets Policy** | Re-registering an existing agent replaces its policy with the global default (no routes), discarding what the policy owner set; found deploying Donna 2026-09-28. | High |
| **GAP-049** | **Admission Builds Pull Anonymously from Docker Hub** | Admission builds pull base images anonymously from Docker Hub from CodeBuild's shared addresses and are refused at the pull limit (429); worked around per agent (SM-donna #6) instead of in the platform. | Medium |
| **GAP-050** | **Infrastructure Creates Secrets** | The AWS landing zone creates secret entries and values, violating K1 and K5 (the Keymaster owns and creates credentials): (a) it creates an empty entry for each gatekeeper-held provider key, so moving `NOTION_API_KEY` to the gatekeeper-egress collided with the existing entry, and the Keymaster could neither create nor supply platform keys; (b) it generates the platform's own tokens (`FACTORY_TOKEN`, `GATEKEEPER_EGRESS_TOKEN`, `GATEKEEPER_INGRESS_*`, `FACTORY_RUN_TOKEN_KEY`, `FACTORY_CALLBACK_SIGNING_KEY`, `FACTORY_TOKENS`, `ECHO_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) with their plaintext values in Terraform state. (b) needs a design decision with the user (bootstrap by the Keymaster; rotating every platform token). Open question: whether the landing zone should grant secret access at all, or the Keymaster. | Critical |
| **GAP-051** | **Invariants Without Complete Checks** | §6.7 requires every machine-checkable invariant to name its test, but most are unchecked or only partly checked, so CI passes code that violates them (GAP-048 and GAP-050 passed every gate). The coverage table in §6.7 lists each one; an independent audit proposes the checks to add. | High |
| **GAP-052** | **Operator Identity Is Asserted, Not Verified** | Violates A1 and A2. The control plane grants roles from the `cf-access-authenticated-user-email` header without verifying `cf-access-jwt-assertion` (`packages/control-plane/src/app.ts`, `authenticate` and `authenticateOperatorOrRun`); an admin email in the header grants admin. The AWS load balancer accepts 443 and 80 from `0.0.0.0/0`, so anyone who finds it skips Cloudflare Access and can set that header; the deploy's definition-of-done checks rely on this, calling the load balancer directly. The console proxies every request with `FACTORY_TOKEN`. Confirmed live 2026-09-29: a forged header with a non-admin email got 200 on `/api/v1/agents`. | Critical |
| **GAP-031** | **Agents Hold Real Secrets** | ECS task definitions inject real secret values into agent containers (e.g. Discord bot tokens, `XAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, GitHub tokens, a username/password pair, GCP service-account JSON), violating S1. Since `6256fac` (2026-09-20) the control plane skips secret binding for non-local runtimes, so it neither pre-flights them (no 412) nor knows their values to redact them from results and the ledger. | Critical |
| **GAP-053** | **Declarations Are Not a Ceiling** | E8: a policy can grant what the cartridge never declared (`PUT /api/v1/agents/:id/policy` and `submind-aws/scripts/agent-deploy.sh` never compare the two; finley's policy grants tool `factory-spend`, which SM-finley does not declare). The Keymaster sees only `secrets.requires` and typed credentials (`packages/control-plane/src/catalog.ts` `declaredCredentials`), so every trigger `secretRef` (`{AGENT}_DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, `DONNA_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) and every per-agent route credential (`{AGENT}_GITHUB_TOKEN` for castle, nick, archie and switch) is never reported as outstanding. archie and switch share an unprefixed `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. Donna's policy exists only in production, not in `submind-aws/policies/`. | High |
| **GAP-054** | **The Perimeter Is Not Yet One Component** | P1: webhooks arrive at the control plane (`/api/v1/hooks/:id`), not at `gatekeeper-ingress`; moving them waits on the webhook decision (agent-factory #32). P2: TSK-046 renamed the perimeter everywhere and `packages/conformance/src/perimeter.test.ts` holds this repository to it, but the SM-* repositories have no CI yet, so nothing checks them. | Medium |
| **GAP-055** | **Policy Comes From Ops-Repo Files** | E7, L4, M2/M3: `submind-aws/scripts/agent-deploy.sh` PUTs `policies/<agent>.json` on every agent deploy, so the operations repository, not the factory, is the source of each agent's policy (routes, models, budget), and a change an admin makes in the factory is overwritten on the next deploy. The dashboard can edit only budgets; routes and models can be set only through the API. | High |
| **GAP-056** | **Dashboard Polling Starves Live Runs** | K3, K5: with the console open, the fleet view re-assessed every agent's credentials each minute (a 30-second summary cache against a 60-second poll), one secret-manager call per declared secret, and refreshed the fleet every 15 seconds. On 2026-10-01 this held the control plane (0.5 vCPU) at 72-100% CPU, so a live run's Google token refresh did not answer `gatekeeper-egress` within its 10-second limit and Donna's calendar and mail calls failed. | High |
| **GAP-057** | **Presence Turns Green Before the Agent Is Ready** | P1: `gatekeeper-ingress` carries Discord presence, and green (online) tells a person the agent can take a turn. It turned green when a wake was merely requested: `gatekeeper-ingress` set `available` as soon as a Discord message woke a sleeping agent, and the control plane notified `available` as soon as the runtime accepted the task (`startRun`, before the container was running). On 2026-10-01 Dale woke Donna from the dashboard; she showed green immediately, he messaged her at 03:19:31 UTC, and her container only logged "waking up" and picked up the message at 03:19:44, 13 seconds later. | Medium |
| **GAP-058** | **Factory State Is Not Backed Up** | R1: the control plane's EFS volume (agent registrations, policies, approvals, schedules, runs) had no backup policy and the account no AWS Backup plan (checked 2026-10-01). Since TSK-048 the factory is the only copy of every agent's policy. No documented or rehearsed restore exists. | High |
| **GAP-059** | **No Shared Skills or Deployment Configuration** | SK1–SK5, E8: there is no skill registry; an agent's abilities come only from its own repo; the factory holds no versioned deployment configuration (registrations and policies are loose files on one volume); admission builds only an agent's own commit; agents cannot adopt a reviewed shared skill without a code change. Also blocks a shared way to narrate progress in Discord (an agent showed only "typing" for 30 s on 2026-10-01). | High |
| **GAP-060** | **Orphaned Policies** | E7, SK3: 69 policy files on the control plane's volume belong to agent ids that no longer exist (each `{"budgetUsd":{"perDay":100},"routes":[]}`), and the TSK-052 migration copied them into the configuration store. The budget and policy endpoints accept an id that is not a registered agent. | Low |
| **GAP-061** | **Schedules Are Not Scoped to Their Agent** | E7, S1: a run token can create a schedule for any agent (`agentId` in the body wins over the caller), list every agent's schedules (higgins' `agentId=` filter is ignored) and delete any schedule. Only donna and higgins can schedule at all; the other agents carry no scheduling code. | High |
| **GAP-062** | **Two Places Choose an Agent's Model** | M2, E7: the console's Models tab offers only `approvedModels` (empty for archie, so nothing can be chosen) and hot-swaps `agent.model`, beside the Policy tab that grants models from the offered catalog (TSK-048). | Medium |
| **GAP-063** | **A Policy Without Models Allowed Every Model** | E7, M2: `gatekeeper-egress` checked models only when a policy listed them, so a policy with no `models` let an agent call any offered model. On 2026-10-01 archie, rosie, switch and compliance-officer had such policies. | Medium |
| **GAP-064** | **Builds Could Send the Source Token to Any Host** | S1, SK1: the agent admission and skill-check CodeBuild builds attached the factory's source token to a clone of whatever `https://` repository a registration named. Since any authenticated user may register a skill (SK1), a registration naming another host would have received the token. Found 2026-10-01 while building TSK-055, which limits its own fetch to `FACTORY_AGENT_SOURCE_TOKEN_HOSTS`. | High |
| **GAP-065** | **No LinkedIn Connection** | K1–K3, E1: the Keymaster serves only Google connections, and its OAuth flow requires a refresh token, which LinkedIn does not issue to standard apps (an access token lasts 60 days). No gatekeeper-egress route reaches `api.linkedin.com`, so Castle cannot post as Dale. | Medium |
| **GAP-066** | **No Hold for Writes in a Person's Name** | E4, E9: approvals cover only MCP tool calls; an `http` route cannot require one. An approval is bound to the run that asked and blocks it, so a review that takes hours outlives the run. Together with GAP-017 a reviewer could neither read nor delay a post an agent sends as a person. | High |
| **GAP-067** | **Agents Can Send Invented Sign-In Links** | K4: an agent directs a person only to the factory's reconnect link, but nothing stops a model from writing its own. On 2026-10-02 Castle's model answered "connect to linkedin and google" with invented OAuth links (a LinkedIn client id that is not the factory's, a placeholder Google client id) redirecting to a third party (`api.emailengine.app`). Castle now builds connection links in code and strips others (SM-castle#7); the factory has no backstop for other agents. | High |
| **GAP-068** | **Systems Are Deployment Configuration** | E10, L1: every system an agent reaches is a gatekeeper-egress route in landing-zone Terraform defaults in this repository (`landing-zones/aws/variables.tf`: discord, github, notion, motion, linkedin, seven Google routes; `landing-zones/gcp/variables.tf` a different, shorter list), extended per deployment by `extra_gatekeeper_egress_routes` and `extra_provider_secret_names`, and loaded once at boot (`packages/gatekeeper-egress/src/main.ts`). A new system needs a platform or ops-repo change and a gatekeeper redeploy. On 2026-10-02 this led a session to add a public X route and Castle's token to `submind-aws/config/deploy.env` (PR #29, reverted in #31). | High |
| **GAP-069** | **OAuth Providers Are Code** | E10, K1: how each OAuth provider works (sign-in URL, token URL, refresh, client secret name) is hard-coded in `packages/keymaster/src/connections.ts` (`CONNECTION_PROVIDERS`: google, linkedin, google-service-account), so connecting a new system (for example X) is a platform code change. | High |
| **GAP-070** | **Grants Carry No Actions** | E8, E9, E11: a route grant allows every method and path on the route, and `hold` matches methods only, so posting and liking (both `POST` on X) cannot be told apart; nothing declares actions or who decides they are held; skill manifests (SK2) declare no actions. | High |
| **GAP-071** | **The Model Offering Is Deploy-Time Configuration** | M3: the offered models are `FACTORY_MODEL_CATALOG`, set by the landing zone, so offering a new model is a redeploy; an agent's unoffered preferred model is not shown as a request. | Medium |
| **GAP-072** | **Rotated Per-Agent Secrets Stay Cached** | K5.5, S1: the gatekeeper-egress caches a static credential under its expanded name (`CASTLE_GITHUB_TOKEN`) but evicts it on a 401 under the route's template name (`{agent}_GITHUB_TOKEN`) (`packages/gatekeeper-egress/src/gatekeeper-egress.ts`, `credCache`), so a rotated per-agent secret is used stale until the gatekeeper-egress restarts. | Medium |
| **GAP-073** | **Agents Name Their Own Credential Entries** | K5.1: a static secret is stored under the name the agent declares (`CASTLE_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `CC_API_TOKEN`), so an agent's declaration decides which secret-manager entry the gatekeeper-egress reads. | Medium |
| **GAP-074** | **AI Sessions Can Weaken Their Own Checks** | §6.7: `main` requires a pull request and green CI but no approving review and no code owners, so a session can change the conformance tests, `baseline.json`, the coverage table or CI in the same pull request as the code they judge, and merge it itself. `submind-aws` and `submind-gcp` are private on a plan without branch protection, so a session can push to their `main` and deploy. The pre-push hook and the Claude Code hook are bypassable (`--no-verify`) and apply only to some tools. | Critical |
| **GAP-075** | **Switch and Archie Cannot Build or Review** | E1, SK2, L3: both cartridges declare a `github-git` route that no landing zone or factory system defines, so every clone and push through the gatekeeper-egress fails; Switch's branch, commit, push and pull-request path was removed from `agent.py` in `39d7153` (2026-09-29) and Archie only reads pull-request diffs. Neither can do the build-and-review work Dale assigned them (2026-10-02). | High |
| **GAP-076** | **Tests That Never Run** | §6.7: a test file that no package test script names passes CI without running. TSK-066's three test files (#63) never ran; one of them failed when first run. | High |
| **GAP-077** | **A Granted Model Cannot Be Reached** | E7, M2: routes and models are separate policy fields, so a policy can grant a model but not the `models` route that every model call uses; on 2026-10-02 Archie's policy granted `claude-opus-4-6` and every call was refused `route_not_allowed`. The console listed `models` as one more route checkbox. | Medium |
| **GAP-078** | **OAuth App Credentials Are Supplied by Hand** | K5.3, K5.1: a provider's OAuth client (`GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT`, `LINKEDIN_OAUTH_CLIENT`) had no write-only path; operators put it in the secret manager by hand under a name they chose, and the control plane could read only those three names, so a new provider (Microsoft, for Higgins's Hotmail) could not be supplied at all. | Medium |
| **GAP-079** | **No Formal Service Taxonomy** | The factory kernel functions as 11 distinct services, but lacked a formal service taxonomy, explicit cross-service contracts, and code-level service attribution, allowing monolithic sprawl and legacy debt to accumulate. | Medium |
| **GAP-080** | **Inter-Service Communications Lack First-Class Wired Contracts (SV2)** | SV2: while the 11 Settled Services (The Cast) are codified in §6.15 and packages are attributed under SV1, inter-service interactions across package boundaries are not yet governed by first-class typed contracts wired directly into real call sites. Private internals risk being accessed directly without mechanical separation. | Medium |
| **GAP-081** | **SV1 Accepted an Empty Directory as a Host** | SV1: `hostedIn` listed `packages/budget` and `packages/triage`, which have no implementation, and the check only required the directory to exist. Treasurer's pricing and circuit breaker live in `gatekeeper-egress` and `control-plane`; Seer's `/metrics` and run progress live in `control-plane` and `gatekeeper-egress`. The pre-push hook (`scripts/conformance.sh`) also did not build `@beercanlabs/factory-contract`, which the SV1 test imports, so a clean checkout failed the hook and a later edit could pass against a stale `dist/`. | High |
| **GAP-082** | **The Cast Test Did Not Read the Cast** | SV1, §6.7: the contract test titled as a check against `DESIGN_AUTHORITY.md` §6.15 compared `FACTORY_SERVICE_NAMES` to a second handwritten array. The eleven names in the document could change and SV1 stayed `checked`. | Low |
| **GAP-083** | **The Treasurer's Rules Are Written Twice and Its Package Is Empty** | SV1, E5, M3: the budget decision exists in two places that must agree: `exceededWindow` in `packages/control-plane/src/policy.ts` (used at `app.ts` ledger ingest and policy update) and `overBudget` in `packages/gatekeeper-egress/src/gatekeeper-egress.ts` (the per-call 402). Spend tallying (`SpendTracker`, `spendDetail`) and `budgetUsd` validation sit in the control plane; pricing (`Price`, `priceFor`, `costUsd`) sits in the gatekeeper-egress `meter.ts`. `packages/budget` is a reservation with no source and a README that describes a caller that does not exist (the real stop is the `BLOCKED_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` run state plus 402s from the gatekeeper-egress, not a hard stop of tasks). A change to one copy of the rule lets the control plane and the perimeter disagree about whether an agent is over budget. A first attempt (`6437f15`, removed in `8a30960`) wrote a new parallel engine (`checkBudget`, a hardcoded `DEFAULT_MODEL_PRICING` table) that nothing called; it contradicted M3 (prices are factory data) and must not be recreated. | Medium |
| **GAP-084** | **An Over-Budget Agent Can Still Be Woken** | P1, E5, §6.15 (Landlord): every wake enters `createRun` in `packages/control-plane/src/app.ts`. Its callers are manual `POST /api/v1/agents/:id/runs` and `/wake`, the `gatekeeper-ingress` wake (its one factory call, `POST /api/v1/agents/:id/wake`), cartridge webhooks (`POST /api/v1/hooks/:id`, which reach the control plane directly), cron and `ScheduleStore` schedules, the SQS queue poll (`queues.ts`), MCP `wake_agent`, and approval delivery. (`routeEvents` wakes `med-doc` and `finops-officer`, which are built-in, not the over-budget agent.) `createRun` refuses paused and isolated agents but never asks about budget, so an agent over its daily or monthly limit is started, then gets 402 on its first model call; on a cold-starting runtime that is a wasted start (measured at 40 to 50 s on AWS Fargate), and the person on Discord sees "starting" and then a failure with no reason. | Medium |
| **GAP-085** | **The Cast Is Extracted One Member at a Time With No Recorded Order** | SV1, SV2: five members (Landlord, Bouncer, Timekeeper, Registrar, part of Seer) share `packages/control-plane` (`app.ts` is about 2,100 lines) and four (Gatekeeper egress, Executive, Treasurer, Seer) share `packages/gatekeeper-egress`. SV1 names which members a package hosts but not which files belong to which member, and SV2 is unchecked (GAP-080), so a change cannot be known to touch one member or one contract. The Treasurer is being extracted first (GAP-083); without a recorded order, contract location convention and rule for parallel work, each later extraction re-decides them and executors collide on `app.ts`. | Medium |
| **GAP-086** | **The Bouncer's Record and Rules Are Spread Over Three Packages and It Has No Package** | SV1, SV2, E4, E9: the approval record (`ApprovalState`, `Approval`, `HeldRequest`, `ApprovalStore`) sits in `packages/control-plane/src/policy.ts` beside the policy store. Its shape is written twice more: `ApprovalState`, `Approval` and `ApprovalConsumer` in `packages/keymaster/src/keymaster.ts` (that copy lacks `kind`, `request` and `notes`), and `Approval`, `HoldRequest` and `HoldOutcome` in `packages/gatekeeper-egress/src/gatekeeper-egress.ts`. The E9 body limit is written twice and must agree: `HELD_BODY_LIMIT` is `192 * 1024` bytes in the gatekeeper-egress and `256 * 1024` base64 characters in `app.ts`. The rule for what makes a held request "the same" (`HELD_HEADERS` and the hash in `holdOrRelease`) is in the gatekeeper-egress only, while the `holds` route in `app.ts` re-validates the same shape by hand. Which requests are held (`route.hold.methods`) and which tool calls need approval (`requireApproval`) are compared inline in the gatekeeper-egress. `packages/bouncer` does not exist; SV1 lists `control-plane` and `gatekeeper-egress` as the Bouncer's hosts. | Medium |
| **GAP-087** | **Authorization Is a Role Name at Every Call Site** | A2, E4, §6.15 (Bouncer): each route names the role it needs where it handles the request, `authenticate(req, res, state, '<role>')`, at 61 call sites in eight files (`app.ts` 34, `credentials.ts` 7, `systems.ts` 6, `skills.ts` 6, `connections.ts` 4, `config-store.ts` 2, `schedules.ts` 1, `events.ts` 1). Authentication and authorization are one function, `authenticate` in `app.ts`. Other decisions are made inline: `hasRole(principal, 'operator')` in `schedules.ts`, `hasRole(principal, 'viewer')` in `stream.ts`, the MCP `TOOLS` roles and the check in `handleMcp`, the ledger ingest's test of whether the caller is the gatekeeper-egress rather than an admin in `app.ts`, and the same test for the connection token in `connections.ts`. The role-implication rule (`hasRole`) lives in `packages/auth`, the Gatekeeper's authentication package. The console carries a different copy (`CloudflareAuth.tsx` `hasRole`: `viewer` is true for everyone, the other roles match exactly, nothing implies anything) behind `usePermissions`. There is no route inventory, so a route moved or added during a Cast extraction can arrive with no check and nothing fails. | High |
| **GAP-088** | **No Agent Owners, and the Requesting User Is Not Known** | E9, K5 item 6, SK3, GAP-037: there is no agent-owner role and no agent has an owner. `POST /api/v1/approvals/:id` needs only the `approver` role, so any approver (or admin) can decide any agent's held action or tool approval with no tie to the agent or to the person who asked. Runs record only the caller's `actor`; the Discord author id reaches the control plane only as `run.input.authorId`, which any operator or admin caller can write, so it is not an identity. K5 item 6 says credentials are addressed to the agent owner "(admins until owner roles exist, TSK-027)"; owner roles are TSK-028 (TSK-027 is admission), and the credentials and connection routes are admin-only. The parties to an action are not stated for any privileged action type. | High |
| **GAP-089** | **Two Access Rules Still Sit Outside the Bouncer** | A2, §6.15 (Bouncer): after the Bouncer flow, the console keeps its own copy of the role rule (`packages/console/src/auth/CloudflareAuth.tsx` `hasRole`: `viewer` is true for everyone, the other roles match exactly, nothing implies anything) behind `usePermissions`, so a button can show or hide differently from what the control plane allows; and an agent's own access (`authenticateRun`, `/api/v1/spend` with the admin-set `factory-spend` policy tool, a run's schedules limited to its own agent) is decided inline, not by the Bouncer. Owner rights are on the REST routes only: the MCP tools stay role-only. | Low |
| **GAP-090** | **Who Holds Which Role Is Deployment Config, Not the Bouncer's Data** | A2, E4, E9, §6.15, §7: TSK-144 implemented Bouncer ingress authorization (`authorizeIngress`) with multi-channel identity links supporting caller names and explicit roles, fail-closed perimeter checks, and authenticated caller injection. Dale's decisions (chat, 2026-10-09): (1) Identity links are Bouncer code: `IdentityLinkStore` and identity types reside in `packages/bouncer/src/identity.ts`, re-exported in `control-plane`, and owned by `bouncer.owns` in `FACTORY_SERVICES`. (2) Clean slate mapping: zero grandfathering for un-roled legacy links; external callers without explicit roles hold no permissions and fail closed at the perimeter unless admin or agent owner. What remains open: migrating identity link persistence out of control-plane file storage into a dedicated Bouncer-owned persistent store (TSK-112), and implementing ingress listener adapters for channels beyond Discord (Slack, Teams, WebUI, CLI). | Medium |
| **GAP-091** | **A Message Into a Live Run Skips the Budget Check, So the Agent Answers "Model Unavailable"** | P1, E5, §6.15 (Gatekeeper, Treasurer; found in production, 2026-10-04): the budget is asked on a wake (TSK-088) and on every model call (E5), but not when the Gatekeeper's ingress hands a Discord message to a run that is already alive (`POST /api/v1/agents/:id/conversation`). Donna, woken by a first message, had her budget revoked in the dashboard; a second message went straight into her live run, her model call got 402 `budget_exceeded`, and her own code (`SM-donna/agent.py`, `UNAVAILABLE_REPLY`) answered "Model access is unavailable right now", which reads as the Executive being down. The person should be told by the Gatekeeper that the agent is out of budget and which limit, and the agent should not be asked. | Medium |
| **GAP-092** | **An Operator or Admin Can Say Who Asked** | A2, E4, E9 (found reviewing PR #85): TSK-106 let any `operator` or `admin` attest the requesting user of a run, on the reasoning that a forged Discord id gives its sender no right. It does when the sender is the person linked to that id: the operator wakes an agent naming their own id, the agent proposes a held action, and they approve it without the `approver` role. Naming someone else's id locks the agent's owners out of that run. Separately, `mayDecide` treated a run that no longer exists as a run with no requester, so any owner could decide an approval whose requester was unknown. Only the Gatekeeper's ingress sees who sent a message, so only it may say who asked. | Medium |
| **GAP-093** | **A Raised Budget Does Not Free a Blocked Agent, and an Agent in ERROR Has No Way Back** | A2, E5, §6.15 (Landlord, Treasurer; found in production, 2026-10-04): when a run is flagged `BLOCKED_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` only the policy save (`PUT /api/v1/agents/:id/policy`) lets it go again, but the console's budget field calls `PUT /api/v1/registry/agents/:id/budget`, which saves the number and leaves the run blocked. Separately, `finishRun` sets the agent to `ERROR` when a working agent's run ends other than DONE, and nothing returns an agent from `ERROR` to `SLEEPING` except a redeploy; a run whose container has gone (for example a result post that timed out) stays active. Donna was left in `ERROR` with no control in the console that fixed it. | High |
| **GAP-094** | **A Message the Destination Will Reject Is Sent Anyway, and the Agent Learns Nothing** | E-routes, §6.15 (Gatekeeper; found in production, 2026-10-05): Donna's replies over Discord's 2,000-character limit were forwarded whole and rejected by Discord with an opaque HTTP 400, so the person saw her go silent mid-conversation although the model had answered. The gatekeeper-egress knows the route and sees the body, but cannot say the message is too long. Intent (Dale, chat, 2026-10-05): the Gatekeeper catches it and gives it back to the agent with the reason; it never splits for the agent, so the agent owns the splitting and the Gatekeeper takes no dependency on it. | Medium |
| **GAP-095** | **The Timekeeper's Rule, Store and Clock Are Spread Over `control-plane` and Reach Back Into It** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Timekeeper): the cron rule (`cronMatches`, `scheduler.ts`), the time-zone and cron validation (`getZonedTimeParts`, `isTimeZone`, `cronIssue`, `schedules.ts`), the schedule record and store (`ScheduleStore`, `checkDue`, `schedules.json`) and the 60-second firing loop (`index.ts`, which is also the composition root) are the Timekeeper's, but there is no `packages/timekeeper`: the code sits in `packages/control-plane`, where `schedules.ts` imports `json`, `readJson`, `requirePrivilege` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts`, and `app.ts` imports `handleSchedules` back. `agentsDueForCron` is typed with the Registrar's `AgentRecord`. A firing starts a run by calling the Landlord's `createRun` directly from the loop in `index.ts`, with no contract between the two. Nothing but `index.ts` and `app.ts` import the Timekeeper's code, so it is the lowest-risk member to extract after the Treasurer and the Bouncer. | Medium |
| **GAP-096** | **The Timekeeper Is Described as Holding One-Shot Wakeup Timers; None Exist** | §6.15 (Timekeeper): the role says "agent-scoped cron scheduling and one-shot wakeup timers", and the cast text (item 9) says it manages "cron schedules and one-shot wakeup timers", but no code, route, store field, test or UI offers a one-shot timer; only recurring cron schedules and cartridge cron triggers exist. A reader of §6.15 would expect a capability that is not there. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-06) that the extraction is moves only; whether to build one-shot timers, or to remove them from the role text, is left open. The other gaps in what an agent can do with schedules (time zone, next run, missed minutes, refusals, edit and pause, visibility) are recorded in `docs/timekeeper-roadmap.md`, not here. | Low |
| **GAP-097** | **The Schedule Handler and `app.ts` Import Each Other** | §6.15 (Timekeeper): `control-plane/src/schedules.ts` (the `/api/v1/schedules` handler, which stays in the control plane) imports `json`, `readJson`, `requirePrivilege` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts`, and `app.ts` imports `handleSchedules` back to route the path. The same shape as the other handlers; it works because the imports are used only at call time. It is resolved when a later cast step moves the routes out of `app.ts`, not by this extraction. | Low |
| **GAP-098** | **The Registrar's Records, Skills, Configuration and Admission Are Spread Over `control-plane` and Reach Back Into It** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Registrar), L3, L4, SK1 to SK5: the agent record and catalog (`catalog.ts`), the registry store (`registryDir/<id>.json`, written inline in seven places in `app.ts`), source pinning (`source.ts`, with `FULL_SHA` defined in the Landlord's `runtime.ts`), the skill registry and checks (`skills.ts`, `skill-checks.ts`), the versioned configuration store (`config-store.ts`), the systems store (`systems.ts`, E10) and the admission logic in the deploy route (`app.ts`) are the Registrar's, but there is no `packages/registrar`: about 3,150 lines in six files plus about 500 lines of registry and deploy routes sit in `packages/control-plane`. `config-store.ts`, `skills.ts` and `systems.ts` import `json`, `readJson`, `requirePrivilege` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts` (`skills.ts` also `applyKillSwitch`), `skills.ts` and `source.ts` import `FULL_SHA` from `runtime.ts`, `config-store.ts` imports the Gatekeeper's `policy.ts` and `identity-links.ts`, and `systems.ts` imports `factory-keymaster`; `app.ts` imports them all back. The Landlord (`runtime.ts`, `runtime-docker.ts`, `runtime-ecs.ts`), the Keymaster (`credentials.ts`) and `aws/codebuild.ts` import the Registrar's record and checks directly, with no contract between them. Roadmap step 4 (the next member after the Timekeeper). | Medium |
| **GAP-099** | **The Registrar's Handlers and `app.ts` Import Each Other** | §6.15 (Registrar): `control-plane/src/config-store.ts`, `skills.ts` and `systems.ts` (the `/api/v1/config`, `/api/v1/skills` and `/api/v1/systems` handlers, which stay in the control plane) import `json`, `readJson`, `requirePrivilege` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts` (`skills.ts` also `applyKillSwitch`), and `app.ts` imports `handleConfig`, `handleSkills` and `handleSystems` back to route the paths. The same shape as GAP-097; it works because the imports are used only at call time. It is resolved when a later cast step moves the routes out of `app.ts`, not by this extraction. | Low |
| **GAP-100** | **Orphan Pruning Reads the Registry and the Gatekeeper's Policy Store in One Function** | SV2, §6.15 (Registrar, Gatekeeper): `control-plane/src/config-store.ts` `checkRegistry` reads the registry directory (the Registrar's data) and `pruneOrphans` archives policies out of the Gatekeeper's `PolicyStore` (`state.policies.archive`) and removes configuration records in the same call; `index.ts` runs it on start. The registry check and the removal of configuration records are the Registrar's, the policy archive is the Gatekeeper's, and there is no contract between them. Resolved by splitting the function along that line, with the Gatekeeper's half behind its own contract. | Low |
| **GAP-101** | **Registration Pins Inline, So the Pinning Rule Is Stated in Two Places** | L3, §6.15 (Registrar): the deploy route uses the Registrar's `pinSource`, but the registration route (`POST /api/v1/registry/agents` in `app.ts`) validates the repository and the commit and resolves a default-branch HEAD (`gitLsRemoteResolver`) inline, with its own `invalid_repo` and `invalid_commit` messages, and the budget, retire, reinstate, purge and model routes change an agent's lifecycle state inline. The task that moved the deploy route's rule left registration alone because its HEAD resolution and messages differ, so a conformance check cannot tell the two uses of the error codes apart (TSK-131). Resolved by a registration contract in `packages/registrar` that the route calls, in a later task. | Low |
| **GAP-102** | **The Observability Member Is Named Seer** | §6.15: Dale chose the name Inspector for the eleventh cast member (chat, 2026-10-08). The name appears in §6.15, in `FACTORY_SERVICE_NAMES`/`FACTORY_SERVICES`, and in `README.md`; SV1's cast test requires the document and the code to agree. Name only; the extraction plan for this member (roadmap step 5) is unchanged and not yet written. | Low |
| **GAP-103** | **The Inspector's Events, Progress, Traces and Metrics Are Spread Over `control-plane` and `gatekeeper-egress` and Reach Back Into Them** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Inspector): the event hub, the ledger tap and the bus sinks (`events.ts`), the per-run progress ring and its sanitizer (`events.ts`), the progress emitter (`gatekeeper-egress/src/progress.ts`), prompt traces (`traces.ts`), the run metrics instruments (`factoryMetrics` in `app.ts`) and the triage rule (inline in the `/api/v1/triage` route) are the Inspector's, but there is no `packages/inspector`. `ProgressEvent`, `ProgressOutcome` and the identifier pattern are defined twice (egress and control plane). `FactoryEvent` is typed with the Landlord's `Run`. `events.ts` imports `requirePrivilege`, `json`, `readJson` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts`. Resolved by the Inspector flow work stream (TSK-135 to TSK-141). | Medium |
| **GAP-104** | **The Inspector's Handlers and `app.ts` Import Each Other** | §6.15 (Inspector): the run-progress routes (today in `events.ts`) and the WebSocket stream (`stream.ts`) stay in the control plane and import `json`, `readJson`, `requirePrivilege` and `FactoryState` from `app.ts`, and `app.ts` imports the progress handler back. The same shape as GAP-097 and GAP-099; resolved when a later cast step moves the routes out of `app.ts`, not by this extraction. | Low |
| **GAP-105** | **The Event Tap Wraps the Auditor's Ledger Store** | SV2, §6.15 (Inspector, Auditor): `tapLedger` replaces `append` on the Auditor's `LedgerStore` instance so each row is also published to the hub, and `hubOf` finds the hub through a `WeakMap` keyed by the ledger. Nothing in the Auditor's contract allows or declares this. Moved as it is by TSK-136; resolved with the Auditor's SV2 wiring (GAP-080) by an append notification the Auditor offers. | Low |
| **GAP-106** | **`/api/v1/metrics` Reads Five Members' Data and Belongs to None** | SV2, §6.15 (Inspector): the route aggregates agents (Registrar), active runs (Landlord), spend (Treasurer), policies and budgets (Gatekeeper) and the ledger row count (Auditor) in one handler in `app.ts`. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-08, D-C) to leave it in `app.ts` and register this gap. Resolved when each member offers a read contract (GAP-080), after which the route can move. | Medium |
| **GAP-107** | **The Inspector Is Described as Routing Crash Diagnostics to Alerting; Only Part of It Exists** | §6.15 (Inspector): the role says it "routes crash diagnostics to alerting", and the README of the empty `packages/triage` placeholder (removed under D-B) described a router to dashboards, ITSM and Slack. What exists is a derived incident list (`/api/v1/triage`), the `system-doctor` built-in agent that reads it, and the bus sink, which ships `crash` ledger rows and run outcomes to EventBridge or a file. No Slack, ITSM or dashboard router exists. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-08, D-D) to register this and build nothing now; the wording is reconciled whenever the intent is next revisited. | Low |
| **GAP-108** | **The Triage Rule Has Two Blunt Edges** | §6.15 (Inspector): the `/api/v1/triage` incident for any failed run with no missing secret and no "timeout" in its error gets category `CRASH_LOOP`, whether or not it looped, and severity is `CRITICAL` only when the error text contains "OOM". TSK-138 moves the rule and pins today's output in a test; changing either is new behavior and its own later task. | Low |
| **GAP-109** | **The Egress Registers Its Own Metrics Instruments** | §6.15 (Inspector, Gatekeeper, Executive, Treasurer): `gatekeeper-egress.ts` creates its request, latency, token and cost instruments inline from the meter `initTelemetry` gives it. The request count and latency are the Gatekeeper's, the tokens are the Executive's and the cost is the Treasurer's, so they stay with their owners; only the OpenTelemetry setup (`packages/telemetry`) is the Inspector's. Resolved when the Executive and Gatekeeper steps (roadmap steps 6 and 8) break up the file. | Low |
| **GAP-110** | **FinOps Visibility Gaps: Cloud Spend, Compute Inventory and Factory Budget Governance** | §6.15 (Treasurer): The factory metered model spend per agent, but cloud infrastructure spend and container compute inventory were not unified into factory reporting, forcing agents to contemplate direct AWS access. The Treasurer must own cloud infrastructure spend, compute inventory reporting, and factory budget governance via control-plane API endpoints, so agents never hold direct AWS credentials. | High |
| **GAP-111** | **A `FactoryState` Built by Hand Fails Only at Runtime Without an Inspector** | §6.15 (Inspector): `FactoryState.inspector` is required (TSK-139), but tests build the state as an object cast to `FactoryState` and `tsc` does not type-check tests, so a test that touches the progress or event routes without one fails at run time. The three tests that needed it were found by running them; a shared test-state helper would make the next one hard to miss. Raised in the review of PR #115. | Low |
| **GAP-112** | **Nothing Calls `Inspector.stop()` on Shutdown** | §6.15 (Inspector): `createInspector().stop()` ends every bus it attached, but the control plane's shutdown path never calls it. Harmless for a process-lifetime object; the contract is only complete when shutdown calls it. Raised in the review of PR #115. | Low |
| **GAP-113** | **Two Copies of the Bearer-Token Reader** | §6.15 (Inspector, Bouncer): `control-plane/src/progress-routes.ts` keeps a local `bearer()` that `app.ts` also exports as `bearerOf` (made public by #113). Moved as it was because the extraction changes no behavior. Raised in the reviews of PRs #114 and #115. | Low |
| **GAP-114** | **The Registrar Lists a `system-doctor` Built-in Agent With No Code Behind It** | §6.15 (Registrar, Inspector): `registrar/src/catalog.ts` lists a built-in agent with `memoryPrefix: 'system-doctor'`, an image name `factory-triage:latest` that nothing builds, `dir: '/app/packages/triage'` (a folder removed under D-B), a `/hooks/triage` trigger nothing serves, and an `/api/v1/triage` trigger that is the Inspector's incident route. It appears in the fleet as a working built-in agent. Not part of the Inspector extraction; resolved by building the alert router (GAP-107) or removing the entry. | Low |
| **GAP-115** | **Cartridge Memory Archetype Schema & Bounds Declaration** | Cartridges previously had no formal contract representation for memory behavior, relying on implicit conventions. Cartridges must declare their memory archetype (`ephemeral`, `episodic`, `workspace`) and bounding parameters (`retentionDays`, `maxMessages`, `maxChars`, optional `prefix`) in `cartridge.yaml` (or `memory.yaml`), validated strictly by `packages/contract` with standard-library reference implementations in `agents/examples/starter-python`. | Medium |
| **GAP-116** | **The Factory Does Not Read the Memory Declaration, and a Run's Memory Scope Comes From Its Caller** | §6.6, A2, E8, §6.15 (Secretary's hydration; Landlord's run start; found in the post-merge review of PR #118): (1) `cartridge.yaml` declares `memory.archetype`, `retentionDays`, `maxMessages`, `maxChars` and `persistence.enabled`, and `packages/contract` validates them (GAP-115), but nothing in the control plane or the shim reads any of them: only `persistence.prefix` is used, fixed when the agent is deployed, and the shim pulls and pushes whenever a memory store is configured. An "ephemeral" agent is ephemeral only because its own code writes no files. (2) The user and project an agent partitions memory by (`authorId`, `project_id` in the starter) come from the run's `input`, which the caller of the wake wrote (GAP-092); the Factory passes the agent no verified scope. (3) One storage prefix and one `$MEMORY_DIR` serve every project of a workspace agent, so a run pulls and pushes every project's files, and runs of one agent still execute one at a time. Per-run or per-scope hydration, a verified scope on the run, and what the Factory does with each archetype are design decisions for Dale and belong with the memory contract of the Secretary and the Landlord; this gap records the problem and is not a decision. | Medium |
| **GAP-117** | **The Model-Service Member Is Renamed the Executive** | §6.15, SV1: Dale chose the name Executive for the model-service member (chat, 2026-10-09): the cast member that gives an agent its access to a model, and so its executive function. The name appears in §6.15, the cast list, `FACTORY_SERVICE_NAMES`/`FACTORY_SERVICES`, `README.md`, the roadmap document and comments; SV1's cast test requires the document and the code to agree. Dale asked that no trace of the old name remain, so it is replaced everywhere, in earlier rows and the roadmap document's filename too. Name only; the role, its files and the extraction (roadmap step 6, not yet planned) are unchanged. | Low |
| **GAP-118** | **User & Project Budgets: Actor-Level Ceilings for Type 3 Workspace Agents** | §6.15 item 7, §6.12: Currently budgets are configured only per agent (`AgentPolicy.budgetUsd`). On Type 3 workspace agents where multiple operators collaborate, a single heavy operator exhausts the agent budget and starves all other users. Spend on the Factory Ledger lacks actor attribution. Needs design confirmation on actor-level budget ceilings via Bouncer identity links and Treasurer actor spend aggregation, followed by project ceilings scoped via GAP-116. | Medium |
| **GAP-119** | **The Executive's Model Service Is Inline in `gatekeeper-egress` and Shares Its Main File** | SV1, SV2, §6.15 (Executive, Gatekeeper), M1, M2, M4, E5, E7: the Executive's code is `models.ts` (catalog, request validation, the Bedrock Converse adapter, `ModelUpstreamError`), `meter.ts` (`SseMeter`, `usageFromJson`, `Provider`, `Usage`) and `sigv4.ts` in `packages/gatekeeper-egress/src`, with its model-policy gate (`modelDenial`) and its factory model API handler (`handleModels`) inline in `gatekeeper-egress.ts`, which the Gatekeeper also owns (`SPLIT_FILES`). There is no `packages/executive`, no typed contract for the policy gate or the call, and no guard against a second copy. Only the egress and its tests import this code. Resolved by the Executive flow work stream (TSK-148 to TSK-155). The model-selection behavior in `docs/executive-roadmap.md` is not part of this gap; it is planned after the extraction. | Medium |
| **GAP-120** | **Skills Have No Visibility or Owner (Public vs Private)** | SK1, SK3, E8: a registered skill has no `visibility` and no owner agent. A skill that belongs to one agent cannot be hidden from the others, cannot auto-adopt for its owner on approval, and every skill in the catalog looks adoptable by everyone. Dale decided (chat, 2026-10-09) the terms are public and private, not "shared"; a private skill still needs admin approval of each version, then adopts for its owner only. | High |
| **GAP-121** | **Skill Manifests Declare No Actions, so HITL Cannot Be Authored per Skill** | E9, E11, GAP-070: `skill.yaml` (`packages/contract/src/skill.ts`) declares `requires` only. The skill author cannot list actions (method and path) or mark each held (HITL) or autonomous, so the approval screen cannot show them and the grant derivation has nothing to read. | High |
| **GAP-122** | **No Adoption: Approved Skills Cannot Be Adopted, and Nothing Records Who Uses What** | SK3, SK5: `skills: []` in all ten live configuration records (checked 2026-10-09) while `tavily-search` 0.1.0, `print` 0.1.1, `discord-progress` 0.1.1 and `factory-schedules` 0.1.1 are approved. No route writes the `skills` list (`config-store.ts` only carries it forward), approval and adoption are one act in practice (the `tavily-search` approval reason names agents), and the registry cannot answer which agents use a skill. Higgins carries its own copies of `tavily-search` and `print` in `SM-higgins/skills`. | Critical |
| **GAP-123** | **A Configuration Change Does Not Rebuild or Redeploy the Agent** | SK4, L3, L4: `factory-agent-builder` builds the agent's own commit only. Nothing builds the agent's source plus adopted skills at their pinned commits, tags by configuration hash, or redeploys when the configuration changes, so adoption cannot take effect without an agent repo change. | High |
| **GAP-124** | **The Console and API Cannot Show Skill Adopters or Manage Adoption** | SK3, SK5, A2: no endpoint lists adopters per skill or skills per agent, and the console has no adopters panel, no agent Skills tab, no retire action and no adoption approval. An admin cannot see which agents use which skill or retire one. | High |
| **GAP-125** | **The Configuration API Shows a Private Skill to Every Viewer** | SK7, A2: a private skill is visible only to admins and to the owners of its owner agent, and the skills catalog and `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/skills` now enforce that (TSK-158). But `GET /api/v1/agents/:id/config`, its history and its versions return the whole configuration record, including `skills` and `skillAdoptions`, to any viewer (`config.read`), so a viewer learns that an agent adopted a private skill and who approved it. Found 2026-10-10 while writing TSK-158. Fix: filter private skills out of those responses for a viewer who may not see them, or move the skills list to a privilege an owner and admin hold. | Medium |

Every caller (`index.ts`, `app.ts`, the three e2e tests) still imports `SystemsStore` from `./systems.js`, so **no `control-plane` file other than `systems.ts` changed and no test was edited.** **Differs from the lock as first written:** `packages/contract/src/services/types.ts` and `app.ts` and `index.ts` needed no change (the file map is unchanged: `systems.ts` stays the Registrar's). **A dependency the plan did not foresee:** the store's content hash is the ledger's `payloadHash`, and it must stay that exact function (the stored `hash` and the ledger row's `payloadSha256` have to agree for every existing record, and the Registrar's own `canonicalJson` in the config store is not guaranteed to canonicalize the same way), so `packages/registrar` now depends on `@beercanlabs/factory-ledger` (the Auditor's package root, which has no dependencies of its own, so no cycle). The Registrar still does not depend on the Keymaster. The lockfile has the one registrar entry changed, and it is identical to what npm generates for it. The ledger is already in the control-plane image and builds before the registrar in every build list, so no Dockerfile, build-list or `conformance.sh` change was needed. **Guard:** `registrar.test.ts` also forbids a standalone `class SystemsStore` (one that does not `extend` something), shown to fail on a scratch edit and then restored; the subclass is the intended one. **Tests:** 8 new in `packages/registrar/src/systems.test.ts` (a proposal's file, hash and ledger row; approve; fall back to the previous approved on a rejection; a reload; list and egress routes; the one-time import of a deployment's routes; a missing directory and no ledger; a subclass opens as itself), listed in the package `test` script; the unedited `systems.e2e.test.ts`, `connections.e2e.test.ts` and `credentials.e2e.test.ts` are the guard for the routes and the Keymaster methods. **Proved:** see the pull request for the full `npm test`, `npm run validate` and `./scripts/conformance.sh`. **Revised after the review of PR #110:** a comment on the two `protected` maps says the control plane's subclass is their only intended reader and that everything else uses `get`, `history` and `list` (comment only). Left as they are, as the review itself suggests: a subclass that copies behaviour passes the line-based guard, and a subclass declared across two lines would be flagged (it fails safe, and the repo does not format code that way). |
